- **Loop 142 (#121 CLOSED) — the first product feature in a while: the decision-detection engine at the heart of Path E.**
  - **What it does:** given several recordings of the same workflow, it finds where they branch and works out the decision there (approve or reject, create or find, and so on). For each decision it gives the outcomes, how often each happened, the conditions behind them, and a confidence score.
  - **Traceable:** every outcome links back to the recorded steps.
  - **Repeatable:** the same recordings always give the same answer.
  - **Review:** an architecture review caught and fixed a real step-matching bug before commit.
  - **Not yet visible in the app;** wiring it in is the next Path E step.

All pass locally. **The next push to `main` is the first real run of this step** — if it fails there, it will be an environment difference, not a test regression. Cadence: 2 of 3 since MR-051.
- **Loop 116 (#295 CLOSED — security): the hold on alerts is lifted.** The public analytics endpoint now accepts only events the app actually sends, can no longer claim to be the server or another user, and is rate-limited. Alerts read only records the server wrote. Website uploads now count as server records too, so the upload alerts see them, and each upload is counted once. **To switch alerts on:** set `CRON_SECRET`, `ALERTS_CHECK_URL`, and one channel (a Slack webhook, or `ALERT_EMAIL_TO` plus SMTP or Resend), then redeploy. Still forgeable, and stated: the SOP-usefulness inputs come only from browsers, and the admin analytics *counts* can be inflated by fake browser events (#252). Web-app 3887 → 3904.
- **MR-051 (Mode 4, after loop 115): HOLD OFF switching alerts on until loop 116 lands.** The public analytics endpoint accepts any event from anyone, and since loop 114 the alerts' memory lives in that same table — so an outsider could silence every alert or make one page every hour. The numbers the alerts are computed from were also forgeable before that. That was my mistake in loop 114. The fix (#295) is next. After it, enabling alerts is: set `CRON_SECRET`, `ALERTS_CHECK_URL`, and a channel (a Slack webhook, or `ALERT_EMAIL_TO` plus SMTP or Resend), then redeploy. Also filed #296 (an alert that flips on and off pages every two hours) and rescored #53 (dashboard component tests gate no deploy).
- **Loop 115 (#293 CLOSED):** about 1 in 10 test runs used to crash with no test failing — and the deploy pipeline runs those same tests, so **a deploy could fail at random for no real reason**. Fixed by running tests in separate processes (40 of 40 clean runs since). **Found along the way:** the deploy's test gate never runs the dashboard's 12 component test files, so a break there would not stop a deploy (#53, now with that consequence recorded). **MR-051 now due (loops 113-115).**
- **Loop 114 (#292 CLOSED):** alerts now notify you **once when they start firing, then at most once a day** while they stay firing — not every hour. They also stay quiet on samples too small to mean anything: under 10 recent signups, or under 5 uploads in 2 hours. **This was the last repo-side step before you switch alerts on:** set `CRON_SECRET`, `ALERTS_CHECK_URL` and one channel, then redeploy. One behaviour to know: a green hourly run now means "nothing new to tell you", not "nothing is wrong" — the alert message (and the daily reminder) is the signal. Web-app 3867 → 3887. Cadence: 2 of 3 since MR-050.
- **Loop 113 (#285 CLOSED — security):** if email is ever unconfigured in production, password-reset links no longer get written to the server log. Email sends to Resend can no longer hang. A send that times out is reported as "outcome unknown", and alerts retry it, so you might get a duplicate alert but should not lose one. Web-app 3860 → 3867. Cadence: 1 of 3 since MR-050.
- **MR-050 (Mode 4, after loop 112):** loops 110-112 hold; nothing reverts. **Two things before you switch alerts on:** the activation alert would page every hour while signups are few, and the check has no memory, so any firing alert repeats hourly — fixing this is loop 114 (#292). **`TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` (#225) now protects three things:** the per-IP limits, the login mass-lockout, and a *silent* mass denial of password-reset emails (#290(7)); set it to your proxy's hop count. One more login trade-off is recorded in #290(6): with that header bypass, one specially found address could make your own logins lock you out after a typo, until the next restart. Corrections: the login-limit tables use ~22 MB, not ~10 MB; and the activation alert reads low by construction (its numerator is a browser event that can be lost in transit), so it leans toward firing. Filed #292 (alert noise) and #293 (a test-runner crash that makes about 1 in 6 runs look red with no failing test).
- **Loop 112 (#250 CLOSED):** the "activation rate dropped" alert could never fire — it compared two different groups of users, so the number only went up as you grew. It now measures one group: of the people who signed up 7-14 days ago, how many opened a SOP within their first week. **Expect it to start firing for real once alerts are live, and to be noisy while signups are few** (one inactive new user out of one reads as 0%). Retention no longer counts too-new cohorts as 0%, and failed dashboard loads no longer count as views. Web-app 3848 → 3860. **MR-050 now due (loops 110-112).**
- **Loop 111 (#277 parts 1-3 done; row now waits on you):** the "Admin" badge in the admin user view now shows who actually has admin access (the allowlist), not an old database flag that no longer means anything; dead bootstrap code is removed and the docs are current. **Decision needed to finish #277:** what should `DEMO_MODE_DISABLE_TEAMS` and `NEXTAUTH_SESSION_MAXAGE` be in production? Today the deploy sets them but they never reach the app — and passing `DEMO_MODE_DISABLE_TEAMS` through as currently written would turn teams off. Web-app 3853 → 3848 (dead tests removed). - **Loop 110 (#291 CLOSED — security):** the login limit can no longer be switched off by flooding it with junk addresses, and simultaneous guesses no longer slip past it (50 at once → at most 5 checked). **The new trade-off:** a very large flood (~10 million requests, then ~700/s) could lock out a large share of *all* users for up to 15 minutes at a time; it requires the per-IP limit to be bypassed with forged headers, which **setting `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` (#225) closes** — that setting now matters for both protections. Web-app 3846 → 3853. Cadence: 1 of 3 since MR-049.
- **MR-049 (Mode 4, after loop 109):** loops 108-109 hold; **loop 107's new login limit has a hole** — flooding it with ~50,000 junk addresses makes it stop counting for everyone else, and simultaneous requests skip it. (2) The deploy's secret rule accepts **only letters, digits and `+ / = _ -`** — a `.`, `@` or `!` stops it too, not only `$`, quotes or spaces. **If the deploy stops at that check, do not use "redeploy" in the Hostinger panel** — the new image is already published and would crash-loop; regenerate with `openssl rand -hex 32` (this signs everyone out). (3) About 20 other deploy secrets (including `SMTP_PASSWORD` and `CRON_SECRET`) pass through the same shell unchecked; if the mail password contains `$` or a backtick, production email may never have worked — the heartbeat will show it once alerts are live. - **Loop 76 (#235 CLOSED; 0 follow-ups; ran alongside MR-039 on non-overlapping files):** the row said establish how gating is enforced *before* deleting — correct, and the answer changed what to delete. **Three live mechanisms:** server-rejects-client-explains (403 + `plan_upgrade_required` + `requiredPlan`, prompt at point of intent); `isGated` payload flags; admin allowlist. **Attempt-then-reject is a deliberate product choice.** `FeatureGate` + `useFeatureGate` + `UpgradeCTA` implemented the **rejected** strategy (pre-emptive hiding) and were dead — the tag appears **zero** times. **Three files, not the two the row named** — `UpgradeCTA` was reachable only through `FeatureGate`, dead behind dead code. All deleted: a complete plausible implementation of a rejected strategy invites accidental adoption and is a second answer to a question that should have one. **Architecture documented in `lib/feature-gating.ts`** — the durable output; the dead files were a symptom of there being no findable answer. **Checked rather than assumed:** `UpgradeCTA` emits `upgrade_prompt_viewed`, so deleting it could have removed funnel coverage — it could not, because loop 70 instrumented the surfaces directly. **Reverse that order and this deletion silently removes the only correct emitter.** Validation: web-app **3278 unchanged** (nothing imported them — that is the claim), typecheck 0, **production build clean** (the check that proves it), validator clean 237.
- **Loop 75 (#243 CLOSED; 0 follow-ups):** ingest loop sat in a single `try` with `await` inside `for`, so the **first** failing row abandoned every row after it — and it returned `received: records.length`, the count *built*. **A batch could lose most of itself and be told it arrived intact.** Now per-row `try` (one bad event costs one event) + honest counts (`received` = written, plus `attempted`/`failed`/`truncated`). 100-row cap stays (unbounded batch is a DoS shape) but truncation is now reported. Outer catch no longer returns `ok: true` over total failure. **Still HTTP 200 regardless** — analytics must never surface as a broken page — and that is the point worth naming: **an honest body and a benign status are separable**, and conflating them is what let `ok: true` mean both "request completed" and "data landed" when only one was true. 5 tests, sabotage-verified (restoring the old shape fails 4). **Fourth defect in this pipeline in six loops** (#238 / #241 / #242 / #243), three of them biased flatteringly; none found by auditing the pipeline, each found while standing next to it. Both now `--content-tertiary` (7.24 light / 5.52 dark), matching `workflows/[id]/page.tsx` which already had it right. **The durable half is the allowlist:** the contrast guard exempted this file's `--border-default` for two decorative separator glyphs and, being keyed by file+token, was silently extending that reason to the star. **An allowlist keyed by file grants a reason written for one thing to everything after it.** Exemptions now declare a count; a third use fails with *"a new use has inherited an exemption written for something else"*. Sabotage-verified. Fixed via refetch-on-mount and pinned by an explicit stale-quota trap test — the cache is correct; **correct caching is the wrong default for a self-invalidating figure**. Now delivers on `visibilitychange` → `hidden`. **A second defect had to be fixed first:** the old unload handler **read the buffer without clearing it**, survivable only while the page was being destroyed; with a visibility trigger it would have re-sent everything on every tab switch. **Trading lost events for duplicated events is not a fix** — same failure, sign flipped. All paths now go through one `drain`-and-send, making read-without-clear structurally impossible. Rule in `lib/analytics-delivery.ts` (13 tests incl. hide→return→hide); e2e stubs `sendBeacon` and drives real visibility transitions — hide delivers once, return delivers nothing, re-hide delivers nothing; sabotage-verified. ## Current state as of 2026-09-09 (SEO/AEO content program)

**Context.** A 10-specialist review (`docs/meta/SEO_AEO_CONTENT_STRATEGY_001/`) was asked to scope content expansion across use case / role / department / industry. All 10 independently recommended AGAINST it: those four axes are already 56 of the 164 published pages (34% of the corpus) and are its weakest third. Verdict was to shift from arguments to artifacts. See `SYNTHESIS.md`.

**Shipped and verified in a production build (commits d090ed5, 65cf278):**

| Item | Outcome |
|---|---|
| Downloadable SOP templates | 17 statically prerendered `.md` files; byte-verified LF-only, no BOM, single trailing newline |
| Internal link graph | 58 orphans -> 0; 91 pages under 2 inbound -> 0; edges 491 -> 640; CI-enforced invariant |
| Install path | `/install` now linked from all content pages (previously zero) |
| Named author + entity graph | Fictional "Research Team" retired across 164 pages; resolvable Person node with bidirectional Organization link |
| `/methodology` provenance | Removed a live false claim (see risk R-1 below) |

Validation: web-app 2790 -> 2937 tests across 160 -> 169 files, all passing; `pnpm typecheck` clean across 11 packages/apps; `pnpm build` succeeds with 17 download files confirmed on disk.

### Open risks

- **R-1 (resolved 2026-09-09).** `/methodology` claimed page data points were "behavioral observations derived from how real recorded workflows actually run." False — all 164 are method claims, and there are zero customers. Corrected in 65cf278.
- **R-2 (OPEN, blocks any reproducibility publication).** `apps/extension-app/src/background/bundle-builder.ts` hashes export bundles with `JSON.stringify`, which is key-order dependent, while `packages/.../canonicalHash.ts` states that approach cannot back a reproducibility claim. All 10 fixtures DO verify against their manifests as produced (coordinator-verified 10/10; a specialist report of "5 of 10 failing" did not reproduce). Net: the manifest proves TAMPER-EVIDENCE, not canonical-content reproducibility. Resolve or narrow the claim before publishing any determinism artifact. NOTE: `bundle-builder.ts` sits under `apps/extension-app/src/background/` and is therefore subject to the Extension Reliability Invariant — real-extension validation required before any change.
- **R-3 (OPEN).** `docs/invariants.md` is stale: states `SEGMENTATION_RULE_VERSION '1.0.0'`, source is `'1.1.0'`. Additional drift on `GroupingReason`, `BoundaryReason`, `RAW_TO_CANONICAL_TYPE` member counts was logged at iter 051 and never corrected. This file is the compaction-recovery source of truth, so drift here is high-consequence.
- **R-4 (OPEN).** The corpus contains no measurements: one percentage across ~850KB, in a code comment; zero external citations. Largest AEO gap. A product branded on deterministic measurement publishes none.

### Content re-entry gate: FAIL on all three conditions

Publishing net-new programmatic pages remains blocked until: average position < 20 AND referring domains > 0 AND >= 1 non-brand click in trailing 90 days. None met. Certification is by CEO/product-manager, never by the agent proposing to publish.

### Awaiting CEO decision

1. Chrome Web Store submission — `chromeStoreUrl` still `placeholder`; ranked #1 by three consecutive reviews; gates every other channel.
2. R-2 canonical-hash resolution — engineering decision, must precede any reproducibility claim.
3. Whether ONE hand-authored evidence page at a new URL is permitted as a bounded gate exception (specialists split; coordinator declined to self-authorize).
4. Tier 2 ownership — G2 / Capterra / SaaSHub listings, founder outreach. Zero engineering. Six of seven prior non-code recommendations were never started while code items shipped within 72 hours; this pattern repeated on 2026-09-08.

**Honest framing:** none of the 2026-09-08/09 work moves average organic position (44). It makes the site worth citing if found; being found is Tier 2, which remains unowned.

## Executive Summary — current state as of 2026-08-19

**The constraint on this business is monetization, not engineering.**

Engineering health is genuinely strong: **4,186 tests across 197 files**, typecheck
clean across 11 packages/apps, production build green, and a real-extension E2E
harness that actually certifies the capture pipeline (4/4) rather than only the
sidepanel UI. Production deploys automatically to a Hostinger VPS via GitHub
Actions on push to `main`.

What is *not* healthy is the path from a visitor to a paying customer:

- **The two tiers that make revenue arithmetically viable cannot be purchased.**
  Team ($249) and Growth ($799) return HTTP 402 and route to a waitlist. Only
  Starter ($49) is self-serve — and Starter ships zero intelligence-layer
  features, i.e. the commodity version of the product.
- **The reason is deeper than the prior P0 lists.** 7 of 8 outstanding
  systems-test P0s are genuinely closed. But `Workflow`/`Portfolio` have no
  `teamId`, `WorkflowShare` is never read by any read path, and
  `effectivePlanFor()` is wired into 2 route files against 17 `.plan` reads. A
  paid Team workspace currently confers **no team capability**. See
  `docs/meta/REVENUE_PLAN_20K/team_workspace_status.md`.
- **Stripe is code-complete but not operationalized** — every `STRIPE_*` var
  defaults empty in `compose.hostinger.yaml`, so checkout returns 503.
- **Organic acquisition is ~zero.** Search Console, 3 months: 1,979 impressions,
  **25 clicks**, 22 of them to the homepage. Average position 44. The 164-page
  programmatic SEO program produced **3 clicks**. Root cause is domain/entity
  authority, not content quality — when a page does rank it converts (one page at
  position 5.0 shows 100% CTR). See `docs/meta/SEO_AEO_EFFECTIVENESS_REVIEW_001.md`.

Full analysis and sequenced plan: **`docs/meta/REVENUE_PLAN_20K_001.md`**.

---

## Current State (2026-08-19)

**Recently closed**
- **Chrome Web Store: all 8 blockers closed** — the listing is no longer gated by
  engineering. Screenshots at verified 1280×800 captured from the real extension.
- **Capture-layer PII fixed and gated** — `document.title` was transmitted
  unscreened in every event; now screened in the content script, behind a
  real-extension harness proven to catch both the PII regression and a
  capture-pipeline break.
- **MRR measurement made correct before there is revenue to get wrong** — trials
  no longer booked as active, Team/Growth included without double-counting,
  annual normalized to monthly-equivalent.
- **Attribution join closed** — `visitorId` promoted to an indexed column,
  `trackServer()` given a pathway it never had, `User.firstTouchVisitorId` set at
  signup. Channel → revenue is now answerable.
- **SOP integrity + off-host backup system** merged after ~4 weeks unmerged.

**Open, ordered by leverage**
1. **Team tier data layer** — 3–5 weeks. The genuine $20k unlock (80 customers
   instead of 408). In progress: making plan gates workspace-aware.
2. **Stripe operationalization** — ~45 min of CEO dashboard work. Hard
   prerequisite for any revenue at all.
3. **`DEMO_MODE_DISABLE_TEAMS`** defaults `'true'` in `deploy.yml` — a flag its own
   runbook says is for the demo environment. May be 404-ing team endpoints in
   production right now.
4. **Chrome Web Store submission** — engineering-clear; needs a human to submit.
5. **Off-page authority** — zero third-party citations vs. every competitor. A
   6–12 month compounding play and the real fix for position 44.

**Known measurement gaps**
- GA4 is **not** instrumented; the stack is PostHog + Umami.
- SQLite single-writer ceiling ~50–100 concurrent writers — stressed first by the
  unlimited-recording Team/Growth tiers that would drive most of $20k.

## Historical detail (iteration ~026 — archive, not current)

Ledgerium AI is in **Phase 1** with a strong deterministic product foundation, comprehensive analytics infrastructure, and clear architectural principles. The current system is strongest in **vision clarity, invariants, analytics coverage, and core trust-first philosophy**. **All three Phase-1 release blockers are now closed** — Playwright E2E (iter 009), session event persistence (iter 010), and LiveStepBuilder ↔ StreamingSegmenter convergence (iter 011). The system is architecturally ready for Phase 2 with no carried blockers.

**Meta-Review 002** (2026-04-19) confirmed MR-001's control changes (formula rewrite, delegation rubric, Mode 5 formalization) are working first-order, but flagged a priority finding: the **density trigger** in `Follow-Up Debt Policy` clause 3 was silently violated 3 consecutive iterations (009, 010, 011 each generated ≥3 follow-ups with zero `density-response` log). MR-002 mechanized the policy into a machine-enforceable log line (Change A), added `Birth iter` as a mandatory schema field (Change B), and introduced a pool-size ceiling rule (Change C) that forces iter 012 to burn-down because the open follow-up pool is 11 items (> 8).

Overall confidence: **High, strengthening further post-iter-020** (3 of 3 release blockers closed; MR-004 Change A companion-burn-down obligation satisfied at iter 019; metrics engine lands pure-deterministic with 66-test coverage expansion). **Post-iter-020:** Path B now 3 of 5 iterations complete (018 PRD + MR-004 ✅, 019 burn-down #15 ✅, 020 metrics engine ✅). Test count trajectory 1646 → 1652 → 1718 across Path B build iterations (+72 net). CEO's Health Score formula (0.30 efficiency / 0.30 consistency / 0.20 reliability / 0.20 standardization) now computable alongside existing v1 per D2 parallel-run directive; Opportunity tagging deterministic via explicit decision tree. Pool 22 → 25 (+3 follow-ups generated iter 020: v1 retirement per D2, stale-chip signature refinement, route sort params) — this is expected during build-heavy PRD execution and `density-response: acknowledged, carried forward` rationale recorded per CLAUDE.md clause 4. **Path B remaining:** iter 021 UI build (frontend-engineer primary, 18 components under `dashboard-v2/`, 5 UI states, `/dashboard?v2=1` flag-gated per D1), iter 022 accessibility + polish + E2E (qa-engineer + frontend-engineer). **Billing revenue-integrity:** unchanged (4.8/5 scorecard; 21-test regression net holds). **Agent diversity:** backend-engineer consecutive-use counter = 2 (iter 019 + iter 020). Iter 021 MUST be frontend-engineer per PRD §8 component build — counter resets. Same-implementer-4+ trigger stays distant. **Deferred governance work (MR-004 Changes D/E/F):** audit-intake codification, reverse portfolio-drift trigger, test-touch counting rule — queued for post-Path-B governance iteration (iter 023+).

**External-launch MDR-blocker gate: 7 of 7 CLOSED — FULL (as of iter 041, 2026-04-24).** All METRICS_DASHBOARD_REVIEW_001 P0 external-launch blockers retired: P01/P02 (iter 035), P03/P04 (iter 037), P05 (iter 039), P06/P07 (iter 034), P08 (iter 041), P09 (iter 038). v2 dashboard is externally-launch-ready pending only the 14d soak window against shipped instrumentation. `#57` flag-retirement prerequisite chain is **engineering-complete (10 of 10 closed)**; only the calendar-time soak gate remains before CEO launch decision per the #57 retirement rule (bounce < 40% AND free-tier p50 click < 60s AND chip-click rate ≥ 10% — all three evaluable with shipped PostHog instrumentation). **CORRECTED 2026-10-01 (MR-040): that last clause was false from MR-038 onward and is kept only so the error stays visible.** Criterion 1 (bounce) became *computable* at loop 80 but is not decision-grade: it is lost on the dominant mobile exit (#249) and can double-count on back-button return (#251), so the net direction is unknown. Criterion 3 (chip-click) is computable, its threshold is still a CEO number, and its net bias direction is also unknown (MR-040 §3.3: toggle-off clicks and error-retry views push it up, lost clicks push it down). Criterion 2 was not re-checked. **No #57 retirement decision should quote these numbers yet.**
